# Price mentions — panamaspot.com

Generated 2026-08-29. Every `$` amount in site content: **2,805 mentions**, **351 distinct amounts**, across **53 pages**.

Source: `public/articles/`, `public/es/articles/`, `public/funnels/`, `src/app/**/page.tsx`.
`out/` excluded (build output). No JSON-LD `offers.price` fields; no balboa (`B/.`) amounts.

A range is one mention (`$20–35`, `entre $3 y $5`, `from $25 to $35`). `+` joins separate fees,
so `$40 + $3 entrance` is two. `[schema]` marks a price inside JSON-LD FAQ data — invisible on
the page, but what Google reads. Schema and copy must be changed together.

---

For beaches and more remote destinations, join a day tour ($25–$45 per person) or hire a private charter ($40–$120 depending on distance).
- **$2–$3** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Is Starfish Beach worth visiting?**  
  Yes, but manage your expectations. The beach is genuinely beautiful — calm, shallow, turquoise water with orange sea stars visible in the shallows. It gets crowded between 10am and 2pm when day tours arrive. Go early on the public bus from Parque Simón Bolívar (about $2–$3 each way) and you will often have it nearly to yourself. Do not touch or remove the starfish — handling them causes harm and is prohibited.
- **$35–$45  $80–$130  $25–$35** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the cheapest way to get to Bocas del Toro?**  
  The bus from Panama City to Almirante plus the water taxi to Bocas Town costs around $35–$45 total but takes 9–10 hours. The flight from Albrook airport costs $80–$130 one-way and takes 45 minutes. If you are already in Boquete or David, a shuttle to Almirante plus the water taxi runs $25–$35 and takes 3–4 hours.
- **$10** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the national park fee and where does it apply?**  
  The Bastimentos Island National Marine Park charges $10 per person for entry. This fee is collected at the ranger station near the Zapatilla Cays and applies to visits to the cays and the protected marine areas. Day tours typically include this fee in their price — confirm before you book. The fee does not apply to Red Frog Beach or Old Bank, which are outside the strictly protected zone.
- **$40–$50** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Can I surf in Bocas del Toro as a beginner?**  
  Yes, but choose your spot carefully. Bluff Beach and Silverbacks are not for beginners — both have powerful shore break or reef break that is dangerous without experience. Beginners should take lessons at the beach breaks on the north coast of Isla Colón or at Carenero Point in smaller swell conditions. Several surf schools in Bocas Town offer two-hour beginner lessons for around $40–$50 including board rental.
- **$25–$45** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the difference between a day tour and DIY island hopping?**  
  Day tours ($25–$45 per person) cover four to five stops in a single day with a guide and typically include Dolphin Bay, Starfish Beach, a snorkel site, and Red Frog Beach. They are efficient and social, and good for first-timers. DIY hopping using shared water taxis gives you more control over timing and lets you linger at places you love, but costs more per leg if you are solo and requires more planning.

Most farms can vacuum-seal bags for travel, and Finca Dos Jefes and Kotowa ship internationally.

- **$6  $8** — Coming from Bocas del Toro, the classic route is: water taxi from Bocas town to Almirante ($6, 25 min), then a shared minibus or bus to David (2.5 hrs, ~$8), then the Boquete bus.
- **$35** — Several operators now offer direct shuttle services from Bocas to Boquete for around $35 per person — worth it if you have luggage or are travelling with a group.
- **$2.50** — For trailheads, the collectivo system is your friend: shared minibuses depart from the corner of Avenida Central and Calle 1 Sur (one block north of the plaza) toward Bajo Mono, covering the Pipeline Trail and Lost Waterfalls trailheads for $2.50 each way.
- **$1.50** — For the Caldera hot springs, take the David-bound bus and ask to be dropped at the Caldera turn-off (~$1.50), then a short taxi to the springs.
- **$15–30** — Budget ($15–30/night): Hostal Nomba and Boho by Arte Hostal Boquete are the two most recommended budget options in town, both within walking distance of the plaza.
- **$15–20  $28–35** — Dorm beds run $15–20; private rooms $28–35.
- **$25** — The Yellow House, 3 km from the centre, offers private bungalows from around $25 and is a solid choice if you have your own transport.
- **$60–120** — Mid-range ($60–120/night): Boquete Garden Inn is consistently well-reviewed for its gardens and breakfast. Hotel Fundadores sits right on the river and has a decent spa. Both are walkable to the centre.
- **$150–250** — Boutique/splurge ($150–250+/night): Finca Lérida Boutique Hotel is the standout — a working coffee estate at 1,600 m with its own trails, resident quetzals, and a restaurant serving estate-grown coffee.
- **$5–8** — Budget $5–8. — From there, walk two blocks to Parque José Domingo Médica , the central plaza ringed with flowering bougainvillea.
- **$30–35** — Finca Dos Jefes and Finca Casanga are the two most recommended operations for English-language tours ($30–35 per person, 2.5–3 hours).
- **$1,000** — Tours cover the full process from cherry to cup and include a tasting flight that typically features the famous Geisha variety, which grows almost exclusively in this highland microclimate and regularly fetches over $1,000 per pound at auction.
- **$39** — Afternoon: walk north along the Caldera River path for views of the valley, then stop at The Perfect Pair Coffee & Chocolate for a late-afternoon chocolate-making class ($39 per person, up to 6 people, book in advance) or simply for a cup and a pastry.
- **$3–4** — For dinner, El Sabrosón on Avenida Central is the honest local choice — a cafeteria-style comedor where you pick from a steam-table spread of rice, beans, chicken, and vegetables for $3–4.
- **$3–5** — Field note · Local eating Beyond El Sabrosón, look for any unmarked comedor displaying a handwritten menu outside — a casado (rice, beans, protein, salad) runs $3–5 and is almost always better value than the tourist-facing restaurants on the main strip.
- **$8–12** — On the descent, stop at any of the roadside coffee stalls selling freshly roasted beans by the bag ($8–12 for 250g of estate-grown Geisha is a legitimate souvenir).
- **$2–5** — Entry is free or involves a small fee ($2–5) depending on which section you access.
- **$10–16** — Return to Boquete by 5 pm and have dinner at RetroGusto or Bistro Boquete on Avenida Central for something more substantial — mains run $10–16.
- **$10** — The first twenty minutes are a steep rocky ascent to the ticket booth ($10 entry, cash only, last entry 3 pm).
- **$2.50** — Take the Bajo Mono collectivo from the plaza ($2.50 each way) and arrange for it to collect you, or simply wait at the trailhead — buses pass roughly every 45 minutes.
- **$15** — The self-guided overnight hike departs around midnight from the ranger station on the Volcán road (taxi from town, ~$15), takes 5–6 hours to the summit, and returns by midday.
- **$5  $15** — Cost: $5 national park entry plus $15 taxi each way.
- **$120–150** — Alternatively, a 4×4 vehicle tour to the summit costs $120–150 per person and is the only realistic option for those who want the view without the overnight hike.

- **$15–18  $2.25  $90–140** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How do I get from Panama City to Boquete?**

- **$10** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the Lost Waterfalls trail like in the rainy season?**  
  The trail remains open in the rainy season but conditions change significantly. The rope-assisted sections on the approach to waterfall three become slippery after rain, and the first steep ascent to the ticket booth can be muddy. Wear trail shoes with grip (not sandals), hike in the morning before afternoon rain arrives, and consider skipping waterfall three if it rained the previous night. Entry is $10 cash, last entry 3 pm.
- **$75** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Can I see the resplendent quetzal in Boquete?**  
  Yes, with reasonable odds if you visit January through April during nesting season. The Pipeline Trail (Sendero Pipa de Agua) and the trails around Finca Lérida are the most reliable spots. A guided birding tour ($75 for two people, half-day) significantly improves your chances. Outside nesting season, sightings are possible but less predictable — early morning is always the best time regardless of season.
- **$30–35** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What does a coffee farm tour in Boquete cost and include?**  
  Most English-language farm tours cost $30–35 per person and last 2.5–3 hours. They typically cover the full process from coffee cherry to roasted bean, including picking, wet and dry processing, roasting, and a tasting flight. Finca Dos Jefes and Finca Casanga are the most consistently recommended operations. Walk-ins are not accepted — book at least a day in advance, ideally online before you arrive.
- **$15  $5  $120–150** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the Volcán Barú hike like and how much does it cost?**  
  The self-guided overnight hike departs around midnight from the ranger station on the Volcán road (taxi from Boquete, ~$15 each way). It takes 5–6 hours to reach the 3,474 m summit and 4–5 hours to descend. National park entry is $5. Bring a headlamp, warm layers, rain gear, and at least 2 litres of water. A guided 4×4 vehicle tour to the summit costs $120–150 per person and is the option for those who want the view without the overnight hike.
- **$3–4  $10–16** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Are there good restaurants in Boquete beyond tourist spots?**  
  Yes. El Sabrosón on Avenida Central is the local comedor chain where a full plate of rice, beans, and protein costs $3–4. Any unmarked comedor with a handwritten menu outside will serve a similar casado for the same price. For something more substantial, RetroGusto and Bistro Boquete both offer solid mid-range meals ($10–16 mains). The Panamonte Hotel restaurant is the special-occasion choice — it has been feeding travellers since 1914 and the farm-to-table menu reflects the highland produce around it.
- **$2.50** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Do I need a car to get around Boquete?**  
  No. The town centre is entirely walkable and the collectivo network covers the main trailheads (Pipeline Trail, Lost Waterfalls) for $2.50 each way. For the Caldera hot springs, take the David bus and ask to be dropped at the Caldera turn-off, then a short taxi. An e-bike is a practical and enjoyable option for the highland farm roads where the terrain involves moderate climbing — E-Valley Bikes operates tours and rentals in the area.

  Chorro Las Mozas is a series of tiered natural rock pools and small cascades about 1.5 kilometers east of Chorro El Macho. Entry is $2 per person. It is quieter, less commercial, and more suitable for a relaxed swim than the main falls. Hours are Monday to Friday 10:00 am to 3:00 pm and Saturday to Sunday 8:00 am to 4:00 pm. It pairs well with Chorro El Macho as a two-waterfall afternoon.

- **$20–30** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is Pozo Azul and is it worth visiting from El Valle?**  
  Pozo Azul is a series of waterfalls cascading into mineral-blue pools in the Coclé countryside, about 30–40 minutes by road from El Valle. It is arguably the most spectacular natural swimming spot within reach of the valley and is largely unknown to international visitors. You need your own transport or a hired taxi ($20–30 round trip). It is worth the effort, particularly on a weekday when crowds are minimal.
- **$10–15** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Do I need a guide for El Valle waterfalls?**  
  El Macho requires no guide — the site is well-managed with clear signage. For Las Mozas, a local guide ($10–15 for a half-day) is strongly recommended in the rainy season when the trail is harder to follow and conditions are more hazardous. For Lovers Falls and Pozo Azul, a guide is helpful but not essential if you have good navigation skills and appropriate footwear.
- **$30** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the best way to visit multiple waterfalls in one day?**  
  Start at Chorro Las Mozas early (7:30 am) to beat crowds and get the best trail conditions, then move to Chorro El Macho by mid-morning. The two sites are about 4 kilometers apart — a 15-minute taxi ride or a pleasant e-bike ride through the valley floor. E-Valley Bikes rents electric bikes from $30 per bike, which makes the inter-waterfall transit easy and enjoyable.
- **$3  $10–15** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Is there a combo ticket for multiple El Valle waterfalls?**  
  No combined ticket exists across the different sites. El Macho is privately managed and sells its own entry. Las Mozas has a separate nominal fee. Lovers Falls and El Escondido are free (El Escondido's $3 fee covers the La India Dormida trail). Budget approximately $10–15 per person to cover entry fees for a full waterfall day, excluding transport.

- **$10** — Historically, rental shops near the market have charged around $10 per bike per day for standard bicycles.
- **$1** — The walk to it is flat and easy, the petroglyphs are genuinely impressive (large carved figures on a boulder), and there is a small entry fee of around $1.
- **$3** — Entry is approximately $3 per person.
- **$4–6** — 7:00–7:30 am — Breakfast. Eat before El Níspero opens. Brisas Panameñas on Avenida Central does a solid traditional Panamanian breakfast (eggs, tortillas, fresh juice) for around $4–6 per person. Café Unido has better coffee if that matters to you.
- **$5–6** — 7:30–9:00 am — El Níspero Zoo. Arrive at opening. Animals are most active in the cool morning. Spend 60–75 minutes. Budget $5–6 for a family of four. Do not rush the EVACC golden frog exhibit — this is the educational centerpiece of the whole day.
- **$12–16** — 9:15–10:00 am — Butterfly Haven. A 5-minute drive or 15-minute walk from El Níspero. Arrive before 10 am when the butterflies are most active. Allow 30–40 minutes. Budget approximately $12–16 for a family of four.
- **$3–4** — 10:00–10:30 am — APROVACA Orchid Center. A 3-minute walk from Butterfly Haven. Spend 20–25 minutes. Budget $3–4 for a family of four. Buy a cold water from a nearby tienda before continuing.
- **$10–15** — 10:30–11:30 am — Market and snacks. Walk or bike to the municipal market. Browse crafts, buy a raspado or fresh coconut, let the kids pick a souvenir. Budget $10–15 for snacks and a small purchase.
- **$3** — 11:30 am–12:30 pm — Chorro Las Mozas. Drive 5 minutes to the waterfall entrance. Pack swimsuits in a day bag. The river trail is shaded and cool. Swim in the shallow pools. This is the highlight for most children. Budget $3 per person entry.
- **$8–12** — 12:30–1:30 pm — Lunch. Return to town. La Divina Comedia does excellent wood-fired pizza in a garden setting — kids' portions available, relaxed atmosphere. Budget $8–12 per person.
- **$2** — 1:30–3:00 pm — Square Trees Trail or La Piedra Pintada. Choose one based on energy levels. The Square Trees Trail is shorter and easier; La Piedra Pintada is more interesting for older children. Both are under $2 entry.
- **$4–6** — 3:00–4:00 pm — Wind down. Pausa Café near the market does excellent tiramisu and fresh-fruit smoothies. This is the decompression hour before the drive back. Budget $4–6 per person.
- **$4** — Field note · Day-trip logistics Buses from Panama City's Albrook terminal to El Valle depart roughly every hour from 6 am, cost around $4 each way, and take 2–2.5 hours.
- **$4–6** — Brisas Panameñas — The best option for a traditional Panamanian breakfast. Eggs, tortillas, hojaldre , fresh juice, and strong coffee. Friendly staff, fast service. Budget $4–6 per person. Opens early.
- **$10–14** — La Divina Comedia — Wood-fired pizza in a garden courtyard. The best lunch or dinner option for families with children who eat pizza (which is most children). Relaxed atmosphere, no rush. Budget $10–14 per person with drinks.
- **$8–12** — Casa Florencia / La Bruschetta — Both are Italian-influenced restaurants that do pasta, pizza, and simple mains. Reliable, family-friendly, mid-range pricing. Budget $8–12 per person.

- **$4–6** — Pausa Café — The best spot for afternoon coffee and dessert. Tiramisu, smoothies, and pastries in a quiet setting. Budget $4–6 per person.

- **$15–25** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Do I need a guide for hiking in El Valle?**  
  Not for La India Dormida, Cerro Cara Iguana, or the hot springs — these trails are adequately signed and safe for independent hikers. A guide is strongly recommended for Cerro Gaital's upper section (trail condition varies) and Pozo Azul Canyon (unmarked route, flash flood risk). Guides can be arranged at the La India Dormida trailhead entrance for $15–25 per person.
- **$4–6  $60–80** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How do I get from Panama City to El Valle de Antón?**  
  Buses depart from the Albrook Bus Terminal in Panama City roughly every hour and cost $4–6. Journey time is 2–2.5 hours. Buses drop you at the El Valle central market, within walking distance of most trailheads. Private transfers and rideshare services like Daytrip are also available for around $60–80 one-way.
- **$3–5** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Are the hot springs at El Valle worth visiting?**  
  Yes, though manage expectations. The Pozos Termales are concrete-lined pools, not a luxury spa. Water temperature ranges from 30–38°C. Natural volcanic mud is included in the entrance fee ($3–5). The experience is authentic and genuinely relaxing, especially on a weekday afternoon after a morning hike. Avoid Sunday afternoons in high season when they fill with day-trippers.
- **$1–5  $10–30** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How does El Valle compare to Boquete for hiking?**  
  El Valle is better for accessibility (2 hours from Panama City vs. 6–7), budget hiking ($1–5 trail fees vs. $10–30 guided tours in Boquete), waterfall swimming, and hot springs. Boquete wins for altitude (Volcán Barú at 3,475 m is the only place in Panama to see both oceans), coffee culture, and overall birding diversity. For a weekend trip from Panama City, El Valle is the clear choice. For a dedicated hiking and coffee trip, Boquete.

- **$3** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Can I hike India Dormida for sunrise?**  
  Yes, and it is one of the best sunrise hikes in Panama. Leave your accommodation by 4:30 am if driving to the trailhead, or 4:00 am if walking from town. You need a headlamp — the lower forest section is completely dark before dawn. The kiosk will be unstaffed; pay $3 on your way back down.
- **$10–20** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Do I need a guide for the India Dormida hike?**  
  No. The trail is well-marked with neon green arrows on posts, and the standard routes are easy to follow. Local guides are sometimes available at the entrance for a negotiated fee (typically $10–20 USD) and add cultural and natural history context, but they are not necessary for navigation or safety on the main routes.
- **$3** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What should I bring on the India Dormida hike?**  
  Trail shoes with rubber grip (essential), 1.5 litres of water per person, sunscreen, a hat, and $3 USD cash for the entry fee. Add a headlamp for sunrise hikes, a light rain jacket in rainy season, and a swimsuit if you plan to use the waterfall pools. Trekking poles help significantly on the wet descent.

- **$17.22  $7.22  $3.00** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How much does it cost to visit the Miraflores Visitor Center?**  
  Adult non-residents pay $17.22. Children aged 6–12 pay $7.22. Children under 6 are free. Panamanian nationals and residents pay $3.00 for adults. The ticket includes the IMAX 3D film and all museum floors.
- **$10–18** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How do I get to Miraflores from Panama City?**  
  Uber is the easiest option — roughly $10–18 from central Panama City, 20–30 minutes. Taxis work too but negotiate the fare first. There is free on-site parking if you are driving. There is no direct metro or tourist bus, though public buses exist if you are comfortable with transfers.
- **$10** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the difference between Miraflores and Agua Clara?**  
  Miraflores is on the Pacific side, 20–30 minutes from Panama City, and shows the original Panamax locks. Agua Clara is on the Atlantic side near Colón (about an hour from the city) and shows the 2016 expansion neo-Panamax locks, which are larger and handle bigger ships. Agua Clara costs $10 for adults and is less crowded. If you only have time for one, Miraflores is more convenient; if you want to see the biggest ships, Agua Clara is better.
- **$165–220** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is a partial-transit boat tour and is it worth it?**  
  A partial transit puts you on a passenger vessel that actually navigates through the Miraflores and Pedro Miguel locks, then crosses part of Gatun Lake before disembarking at Gamboa. It costs $165–220 per person and takes 6–8 hours including transfers. It is significantly more expensive than the visitor centre but delivers a completely different experience — you are inside the locks, watching the water rise around you. Most operators run on Saturdays; book at least a week ahead in dry season.

In practice, showing a return or onward ticket is more important. Check current entry requirements for your nationality before travelling.
- **$20  $10  $165–$185  $250–$350** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How much does it cost to see the Panama Canal at Miraflores?**  
  Entry to the Miraflores Visitor Centre is $20 for adults and $10 for children. It's open daily 8 a.m.–5 p.m. Partial transit boat tours (sailing through one or two locks) cost $165–$185 per person and run on select Saturdays. Full transit tours are $250–$350 and run on Fridays and Saturdays. Book transit tours well in advance.
- **$65** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the best neighborhood to stay in Panama City for a 3-day trip?**  
  Casco Viejo is the most atmospheric choice — you're walking distance from the best restaurants, rooftop bars, and the sea wall. El Cangrejo is the best value base, with mid-range hotels from $65/night and easy Uber access to everything. Avoid staying in Punta Pacífica or San Francisco unless you specifically want tower-block luxury — they're safe but characterless and far from the main sights.
- **$18–$25  $15** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How do I get from Tocumen Airport to the city?**  
  Uber is the easiest option: $18–$25 to Casco Viejo or El Cangrejo, 30–45 minutes in normal traffic. The Metro reaches Tocumen (Line 2) but requires a line change at San Miguelito and doesn't serve Casco Viejo — only worth it if you're staying near a metro station and want to save $15. Avoid unmarked taxis at the airport arrivals hall.
- **$22** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Is the Biomuseo worth visiting?**  
  Yes, particularly if you're interested in natural history or architecture. Frank Gehry's building is spectacular, and the eight permanent galleries explain Panama's biodiversity and geological history in a genuinely engaging way. Entry is $22 adults. It's closed on Mondays, so plan accordingly. Allow 90 minutes inside.
- **$4  $75–$85** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What should I eat in Panama City?**  
  Start with ceviche de corvina at the Mercado de Mariscos ($4, the city's best cheap meal). Try sancocho (a hearty chicken and yam soup, Panama's national dish) at a local fonda. For upmarket Panamanian cuisine, Donde José in Casco Viejo is the city's best restaurant (book weeks ahead, $75–$85 tasting menu). Café Unido in Casco Viejo serves excellent Geisha coffee from the highlands of Boquete.
- **$4–$5  $80–$100** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Can I do a day trip to El Valle de Antón from Panama City?**  
  Yes. El Valle de Antón is about 2.5 hours west of Panama City. Public buses run from the Albrook terminal for $4–$5 each way. Private transfers cost $80–$100 each way. The Sunday artisan market is the main draw, but the crater village has hot springs, waterfall hikes, and cool mountain air year-round. It makes an excellent Day 3 alternative if you've already done the Canal and Casco Viejo.

- **$30** — From $30/bike · Beginner & family friendly

- **$4–$6** — For budget eating, the fondas around the market serve set lunches for $4–$6.
- **$4.50** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How do I get from Panama City to El Valle de Antón by bus?**  
  Take a bus from Albrook Grand Transport Terminal (Platform 48) directly to El Valle. Buses depart roughly every 30–45 minutes from 6:00 am to 6:00 pm. The fare is $4.50 each way and the journey takes about 2.5 hours. Buy your ticket at the terminal window before boarding. The last return bus from El Valle departs around 5:00 pm.
- **$5** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Where can I see the Panamanian golden frog?**  
  The golden frog is functionally extinct in the wild. Your only realistic chance to see one is at the El Valle Amphibian Conservation Center (EVACC), located inside El Níspero Zoo. Admission is included in the $5 zoo entrance fee. The center is part of the Panama Amphibian Rescue and Conservation Project and houses the last captive population of this species.
- **$48–$76  $145–$230  $130–$160** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What does a trip to El Valle de Antón cost?**  
  Budget travellers can manage on $48–$76 per day including hostel accommodation, fonda meals, and trail entrance fees. Mid-range travellers (private guesthouse, restaurant meals, one guided tour) should budget $145–$230 per day. A two-night budget trip from Panama City — bus, accommodation, all activities — runs approximately $130–$160 all-in.
- **$15–$20** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Are trails in El Valle de Antón well marked?**  
  Inconsistently. La India Dormida has markers but they are easy to miss, particularly on the upper section. Cerro La Silla is more straightforward. For Cerro Cara Iguana and Cerro Gaital, a local guide is strongly recommended — the trails are poorly marked and the forest is dense. Guides are available at the La India Dormida trailhead for $15–$20, or can be arranged through your hotel.
- **$3–$5** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the best way to get around El Valle de Antón?**  
  The town centre is walkable. For the wider caldera — trailheads, waterfalls, hot springs, and conservation sites — a bicycle or e-bike is the most practical option, letting you chain multiple stops in a single morning. Local taxis charge $3–$5 for most in-town trips. E-Valley Bikes offers guided and self-guided e-bike tours that cover the key sites efficiently. Rental cars are useful if you are doing day trips beyond the valley.
- **$12–$18  $14–$22  $4–$6** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Are there good restaurants in El Valle de Antón?**  
  Better than most guides suggest. La Ranita Gourmet is the top all-rounder (Panamanian and European dishes, $12–$18 mains). La Divina Commedia serves excellent house-made pasta and pizza ($14–$22). Ruta 71 is the casual beer-and-burger spot. Kare Coffee is the morning coffee destination. For budget eating, the fondas around the market serve set lunches for $4–$6.

- **$75–$85  $120–$155** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is the Volcán Barú hike like?**  
  The overnight summit hike gains 2,200 metres over 13 km and takes six to eight hours to ascend. It is steep, cold (temperatures near 0°C at the summit), and dark for the first half. Most people hire a guide ($75–$85). A 4x4 vehicle ascent ($120–$155) is available for those who want the views without the physical effort.
- **$5  $10** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Are there free things to do in Boquete?**  
  Yes. The central plaza, the Caldera River bridge, and the Tuesday market are all free. The El Pianista Trail has no entrance fee. Walking the Bajo Boquete loop through the flower nurseries and coffee farms costs nothing. The Pipeline Trail ($5) and Lost Waterfalls ($10) are the cheapest paid activities.
- **$4–$8** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Is it worth renting a car in Boquete?**  
  A car is useful but not essential. Taxis are cheap ($4–$8 to most trailheads), reliable, and the drivers know the area well. For exploring the valley independently — visiting multiple coffee farms, reaching lesser-known trails, or making spontaneous stops — an e-bike rental from E-Valley Bikes is often more practical and more enjoyable than a car, particularly for the Jaramillo road loop.

A licensed bilingual guide leads the 3-hour circuit covering the EVACC amphibian centre, artisan market, petroglyphs, and scenic viewpoints. Lunch is not included.
- **$65** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Is the zip-line at Chorro El Macho worth it?**  
  Yes, if zip-lining is something you enjoy. Canopy Adventure runs four cables above the cloud forest and over the waterfall for $65 per person. It takes about 1.5 hours. Note that doing the zip-line on a day tour from Panama City usually means skipping other activities — there is not time for both the zip-line and a full hike on the same day.
- **$2–$6** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What is El Níspero Zoo and is it worth visiting?**  
  El Níspero is a small zoo and botanical garden with around 90 species including the critically endangered Panamanian golden frog, tapirs, and raptors. Entrance is $2–$6. It is worth 1–2 hours, especially for families with children or anyone interested in the golden frog conservation project. The orchid garden is one of the best in Panama.
- **$3–$8** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Are there hot springs in El Valle de Antón?**  
  Yes. The geothermal hot springs (Pozas Termales) are on the eastern edge of town, a short taxi or bike ride from the centre. There are two sites — a basic public facility and a slightly more developed private one. Entrance is $3–$8. The water is warm and therapeutic rather than scalding. Best in the late afternoon.

- **$30–$45** `[schema]` — **FAQ — What are the best tours in Boquete for first-time visitors?**  
  Start with a guided coffee farm tour ($30–$45) on your first morning to understand the landscape and local culture, then add an e-bike tour on the Bajo Mono Loop in the afternoon or the following day. If you have three or more days, add the Lost Waterfalls hike and a rafting day on the Chiriquí Nuevo River. This combination covers Boquete's core experiences without overloading your schedule.
- **$30–$35  $55–$65  $40–$55** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How do Boquete coffee tours differ from each other?**  
  The main differences are group size, depth of education, and tasting quality. Budget tours ($30–$35) at working farms like Finca Dos Jefes give you a solid cherry-to-cup overview in groups of 8–12. Mid-range specialist tours ($55–$65) limit groups to 4 people, include professional cupping with multiple varietals, and are led by certified baristas or Q-graders. The roast-your-own format ($40–$55) is best for coffee enthusiasts who want a technical session rather than a farm walk. None accept walk-ins — book at least 24 hours ahead.
- **$130–$200  $35–$45  $55–$75  $55–$65  $55–$80  $10–$15  $115** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How much should I budget for two days of tours in Boquete?**  
  A realistic two-day budget for tours (excluding accommodation and meals) is $130–$200 per person. Day one: coffee farm tour ($35–$45) plus a guided e-bike tour with E-Valley Bikes ($55–$80). Day two: rafting on the Chiriquí Nuevo ($55–$65) or a zip-line canopy tour ($55–$75). Add 7% ITBMS tax and $10–$15 per day in tips. Multi-day combo packages from operators like Boquete Outdoor Adventures start at $115 for two days and can offer better value if the included activities match your interests.
- **$30–$45** `[schema]` — **FAQ — Can I visit Boquete coffee farms without a tour?**  
  Most farms, including Finca Dos Jefes and Finca Lérida, do not accept unannounced walk-in visitors. You need to book a tour slot in advance. A few farms have on-site cafés where you can buy coffee without a formal tour, but you will not access the processing facilities or receive any education without a booking. If you have a rental car, you can self-drive to farms and attend their scheduled morning or afternoon tour slots ($30–$45) without needing a transport add-on.
- **$2–$35  $9  $7  $120–$180** `[schema]` — **FAQ — How do I get from Boquete to Bocas del Toro?**  
  There is no direct public transport. The most common route is: taxi or bus from Boquete to David ($2–$35), then a bus from David to Almirante ($9, 3.5 hours), then a water taxi from Almirante to Bocas Town ($7, 25 minutes). Total journey time is 5–6 hours. Some operators offer private transfers from Boquete directly to Bocas del Toro for $120–$180 per vehicle, which is worth considering for groups of three or more.

  Not for the Boquete route — it follows a single jeep track with distance markers and you cannot get lost. A guide is recommended for solo hikers, first-time high-altitude hikers, and anyone attempting the Los Llanos (western) route, where navigation is genuinely difficult. Guided hikes cost $60–$90 per person including transport.

  Se puede ir y volver en el día cómodamente si sales temprano de Ciudad de Panamá. Pero si quieres hacer la India Dormida además del canopy y la cabalgata, o si quieres evitar el tráfico de regreso del domingo, quedarte una noche vale la pena. El Hotel Campestre y Casa de Campo son las opciones más establecidas; hay también hostales económicos en el centro del pueblo desde $25-40 USD por noche.

- **$15** — La búsqueda de "restaurantes en Casco Viejo económicos" es una de las más frecuentes, y la respuesta existe aunque no siempre está en los listados de los grandes medios. Hay opciones reales por debajo de $15 por persona, incluida bebida.
- **$8–$12  $4** — La Rana Dorada (Av. B, cerca de Calle 1a): La cervecería artesanal más conocida de Panamá tiene una sucursal en Casco Viejo. Pinta de cerveza artesanal desde $4, hamburguesas y alitas entre $8 y $12. Sin pretensiones, con buen ambiente local. Abre desde el mediodía.
- **$10–12  $12** — Tacos La Neta: Tacos mexicanos auténticos en un local pequeño y sin reservas. Tres tacos con bebida salen por unos $10–12. Es uno de los pocos lugares donde puedes comer sentado por menos de $12 en la zona. Cierra temprano (hacia las 9pm), así que es mejor para cenar pronto.
- **$3–4  $1** — Comida callejera en Avenida Central: Los vendedores ambulantes cerca de la entrada del barrio ofrecen ceviche de corvina en vaso por $3–4, empanadas fritas por $1 y raspados de frutas tropicales.
- **$10–13** — Mahalo: Poke bowls y wraps saludables con ingredientes frescos. Precio medio de $10–13. Tiene opciones vegetarianas y veganas claras en el menú, lo que lo convierte en una de las pocas opciones accesibles para quienes no comen carne.

## /es/articles/cinta-costera-panama-mercado-mariscos-panama-viejo
`public/es/articles/cinta-costera-panama-mercado-mariscos-panama-viejo.html` · ES · Article · 52 mentions

- **$15** — Tres kilómetros de malecón, el ceviche más famoso del país y las ruinas de la primera ciudad europea del Pacífico americano: esto es lo que ofrece el frente costero de Ciudad de Panamá en menos de medio día y por menos de $15 por persona.
- **$12–18** — El costo total — ceviche, entrada al museo de Panamá Viejo y transporte — ronda los $12–18 por persona.
- **$1–1,50** — El primer kilómetro tiene estaciones de ejercicio, el Jardín Japonés (pequeño pero agradable, con tortuga y koi) y puestos de raspao — hielo molido con leche condensada y sirope de frutas — que cuestan $1–1,50.
- **$4** — Al final del viaducto podés entrar directamente al Casco Antiguo o continuar hacia El Chorrillo, donde el Fritódromo sirve pescado frito con patacón desde $4.
- **$3–5** — Hay puestos de alquiler de bicicletas comunes cerca del Mercado de Mariscos por $3–5/hora.
- **$1** — Estacionamiento: hay playa de estacionamiento soterrada en la Fase II (cerca del Mercado) por $1/hora.
- **$3–5** — Una porción individual cuesta $3–5 dependiendo del puesto.
- **$6–9** — Filete de corvina frito con patacón: $6–9. El plato más pedido después del ceviche. El patacón (plátano verde aplastado y frito) absorbe el aceite de la fritura de manera que resulta adictivo.
- **$8–12** — Guacho de mariscos: $8–12. Un arroz caldoso con camarones, almejas y cangrejo — piensen en un risotto tropical. Tarda más en prepararse pero vale cada minuto.
- **$5–7** — Ceviche de camarón: $5–7. Más suave que el de corvina, con un toque dulce. Buena opción si el ají chombo te parece demasiado.
- **$5–8** — Patacón con camarones: $5–8. Patacón grande relleno de camarones salteados con ajo y tomate. Contundente y barato.
- **$15–25** — Hay también una sección de restaurantes más formales en el piso superior del edificio principal, con precios más altos ($15–25 por plato) y aire acondicionado. No son necesariamente mejores — simplemente más cómodos para quien no tolera el calor.
- **$10–15** — Presupuesto realista: Ceviche + plato principal + bebida = $10–15 por persona en los puestos informales. El doble en los restaurantes del piso superior.
- **$15  $10  $5** — Entrada: $15 para extranjeros (incluye museo y sitio arqueológico). $10 para residentes. Niños menores de 12 años: $5. Abierto martes a domingo, 9 am a 5 pm. Cerrado los lunes. Hay estacionamiento gratuito en el sitio.
- **$5–8** — Desde el Mercado de Mariscos, la opción más práctica es Uber o taxi: 15–20 minutos en tráfico normal, $5–8 en Uber.
- **$0,35  $4** — Miraflores / Albrook | Metro L1 → Est. 5 de Mayo + Uber | $0,35 + $4 | 25 min | Económico
- **$3–5** — Punta Paitilla / Bella Vista | A pie o Uber corto | $3–5 | 10 min | Fácil
- **$18–25** — Aeropuerto Tocumen | Uber directo | $18–25 | 30–45 min | Caro pero directo
- **$0,35** — El Metrobús (sistema de buses urbanos, tarifa $0,35 con tarjeta Rapipass) tiene varias rutas que recorren la Avenida Balboa paralela a la Cinta. Buscá rutas con destino "Albrook" o "Paitilla" según la dirección. La app Moovit muestra las rutas en tiempo real.
- **$1** — Estacionamiento propio: Si venís en auto, el estacionamiento soterrado de la Fase II (junto al Mercado de Mariscos) cobra $1/hora y tiene capacidad suficiente entre semana. Los fines de semana se llena antes de las 9 am.
- **$5–8** — Uber desde Panamá Viejo al Mercado: 15–20 minutos, $5–8. Llegás cuando el mercado está en su momento de mayor frescura y antes de la avalancha de turistas del mediodía.
- **$3–5  $6–9** — Pedí el ceviche de corvina ($3–5) y, si tenés hambre, un filete frito con patacón ($6–9). Calculá 45–60 minutos. Pagá en efectivo — no todos los puestos aceptan tarjeta.
- **$15  $12  $13  $4  $44  $35** — Nota de campo · Presupuesto total Entrada Panamá Viejo ($15) + ceviche y plato en el Mercado ($12) + Uber x2 ($13) + bebidas ($4) = aproximadamente $44 por persona. Si venís en pareja y compartís platos en el Mercado, bajá a $35 por persona.
- **$3** — $3 — El ceviche más barato del país

- **$150–$200** — En temporada alta (diciembre–abril, Semana Santa, feriados) los precios suben a $150–$200 o más.

- **$33–$38  $100–$200** — Esta es la ruta del viajero con presupuesto ajustado y es perfectamente viable. El total entre bus y lancha ronda los $33–$38 por persona, frente a los $100–$200 del vuelo. El precio que pagas es el tiempo: entre 10 y 12 horas de viaje nocturno.

- **$8–$15** — Desde hoteles en Miraflores, Casco Viejo o el centro, un taxi ronda los $8–$15.

- **$5–$10** — Parking en Almirante: Hay varios estacionamientos privados cerca del muelle que cobran entre $5 y $10 por día. No son vigilados las 24 horas en todos los casos; pregunta al encargado antes de dejar el coche varios días. Deja el mínimo de objetos de valor visibles dentro del vehículo.

- **$5** — La entrada cuesta $5 por persona.

- **$3–5** — Un conjunto de pozas de agua termal de origen volcánico, a temperatura de unos 38–40 °C. La entrada es de $3–5. - **$4  $1  $10** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Qué hacer en El Valle de Antón gratis o casi gratis?**  
  La caminata a La India Dormida es libre (solo pagas el taxi al trailhead, ~$4). La Piedra Pintada tiene acceso libre. Chorro Las Mozas cuesta $1. Recorrer el pueblo y el mercado no tiene costo. El Mirador Cerro de la Cruz es de acceso libre. Con menos de $10 puedes tener un día muy completo.
- **$3  $3–5  $2** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Cómo me muevo entre los atractivos dentro de El Valle?**  
  El centro del pueblo, el mercado, los pozos termales y el zoológico están a menos de 20 minutos caminando entre sí. Para llegar a las cascadas y los trailheads de senderismo, las opciones son: taxi local ($3–5 por carrera), mototaxi (~$2), bicicleta convencional (alquiler desde $3/día en hoteles) o e-bike con E-Valley Bikes, que es la opción más cómoda para cubrir varios puntos en un día sin llegar agotado.
- **$7  $4  $8** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Hay aguas termales en El Valle de Antón?**  
  Sí. Los Pozos Termales (también llamados Ojo de Agua) están a pocos minutos del centro. Hay tres pozas de diferentes temperaturas y zona de barro volcánico. Entrada: $7 adultos, $4 niños. El El Valle Resort también tiene aguas termales privadas en su pasadía desde $8. Abiertos todos los días.
- **$5** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Qué es la rana dorada de Panamá y dónde verla?**  
  La rana dorada (Atelopus zeteki) es el símbolo nacional de Panamá y una especie en peligro crítico de extinción. En la naturaleza prácticamente ha desaparecido por una bacteria (quitridiomicosis). El Centro de Conservación de Anfibios de El Valle (EVACC), dentro del Zoológico El Níspero, es uno de los pocos lugares del mundo donde puedes verla viva. La entrada al zoológico cuesta $5 adultos extranjeros.

## /es/articles/que-hacer-en-ciudad-de-panama

- **$8** — Esclusas de Miraflores | Canal de Panamá, centro de visitantes | 20–25 min en Uber (~$8) | Mañana completa
- **$10** — Panamá Viejo | Ruinas arqueológicas, Torre de la Catedral | 25–30 min en Uber (~$10) | Medio día
- **$7** — Parque Metropolitano | Senderismo en selva urbana, aves, miradores | 15–20 min en Uber (~$7) | Mañana
- **$6** — Marbella / San Francisco | Restaurantes locales, vida cotidiana, cafés | 15 min en Uber (~$6) | Cena o almuerzo

- **$5–12** — Un trayecto típico entre zonas cuesta $5–12.
- **$0.35** — El Metro (Líneas 1 y 2) es barato ($0.35 por viaje con tarjeta Metrobús) y útil para ir desde Albrook hasta el centro, pero no llega a Casco Viejo ni a Amador.
- **$2** — Plaza de la Independencia (Plaza Mayor): El corazón del barrio, flanqueado por la Catedral de Santa María la Antigua, el Museo del Canal Interoceánico ($2 entrada) y el Palacio Municipal. Llega antes de las 9 a.m. para verla casi vacía y con buena luz fotográfica.
- **$4–6** — Es el lugar más honesto y barato para comer ceviche en la ciudad: un plato de ceviche de corvina con patacones cuesta $4–6.
- **$12–20** — El piso de arriba tiene restaurantes con vistas a la bahía a precios de $12–20 por plato.
- **$85** — Donde José: El restaurante más reconocido del Casco Viejo , con menú degustación de cocina panameña contemporánea. Reserva con semanas de antelación. Menú completo ~$85 por persona sin vinos.
- **$4–6** — Café Unido: La mejor cadena de café de especialidad del país, con varias sucursales en el Casco. Un espresso de café geisha panameño cuesta $4–6. Ideal para el desayuno antes de recorrer el barrio.
- **$3** — Rooftop del Selina Casco Viejo: La terraza más accesible del barrio para tomar una Balboa (la cerveza nacional, ~$3) con vistas al skyline y a la bahía. Ambiente joven e internacional, abierto hasta tarde.
- **$8** — Las Esclusas de Miraflores están a 20–25 minutos en Uber desde el Casco Viejo (~$8).
- **$20  $10** — La entrada cuesta $20 para adultos y $10 para niños (menores de 5 años gratis).
- **$45–60** — Si tienes coche de alquiler o contratas un tour que incluya transporte (~$45–60 por persona), vale la pena combinar con una visita a Portobelo o Gamboa en el mismo día.
- **$165** — Operadores como Canal y Bay Tours ofrecen este recorrido los sábados por ~$165 por adulto (medio día).
- **$3–5** — El paseo en bicicleta por la Calzada es una de las actividades más agradables y económicas de la ciudad: alquila una bici en los puestos cerca de la entrada por $3–5 la hora. El recorrido completo de ida y vuelta son unos 6 km a ritmo tranquilo, con paradas para fotografiar el Canal y los barcos en espera.
- **$22  $11** — Entrada: $22 adultos, $11 niños.
- **$10  $5** — El sitio arqueológico tiene un buen museo de sitio ($10 adultos, $5 niños) con piezas originales y maquetas de cómo lucía la ciudad en su apogeo.
- **$10** — Dedica 2–3 horas a Panamá Viejo. Llega antes de las 9 a.m. para evitar el calor y los grupos de cruceristas. Está a 25–30 minutos en Uber desde el Casco Viejo (~$10).
- **$5  $2** — Entrada: $5 adultos, $2 niños.
- **$15–60  $20–80** — Encontrarás molas kunas (paneles de tela con diseños geométricos, $15–60 según tamaño y complejidad), sombreros pintados de la Península de Azuero ($20–80), tagua tallada y cerámica precolombina.
- **$7** — El centro comercial más premium de la ciudad, en el barrio de Punta Pacífica. Tiene marcas internacionales (Zara, H&M, Apple Store), restaurantes de cadena y cines. - **$10** — Tantalo Hotel Rooftop: El rooftop más conocido del Casco Viejo, en la Calle 8ª. Cócteles desde $10, vistas de 360° al skyline y a la bahía. Llena entre las 8 y las 11 p.m. los jueves, viernes y sábados. Sin reserva, pero puede haber espera.
- **$12** — Rooftop del American Trade Hotel: Más elegante y tranquilo que el Tantalo. El hotel es un edificio art déco restaurado de los años 1920; la terraza tiene jazz en vivo algunos jueves. Cócteles desde $12.
- **$3** — Selina Casco Viejo: La opción más casual y económica, con ambiente mochilero internacional. Balboa fría desde $3, vistas decentes y música hasta tarde.

- **$20–$30** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Cuál es la diferencia entre un tour privado y uno compartido en El Valle?**  
  La mayoría de los operadores locales en El Valle trabajan con grupos privados por defecto, especialmente los guías independientes de senderismo y e-bike. Esto significa que el tour se adapta a tu ritmo y puedes pedir paradas adicionales. Los tours desde Ciudad de Panamá pueden ser compartidos (más económicos, menos flexibles) o privados ($20–$30 más por persona). Para actividades de naturaleza, el formato privado vale la diferencia.

- **$10–$15** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Cuál es la diferencia entre el café Geisha y el café estándar de Boquete?**  
  El café Geisha es una variedad de la especie Coffea arabica con un perfil aromático completamente distinto al café estándar: notas de jazmín, bergamota, melocotón y té. Crece bien solo por encima de los 1.500 metros de altitud y produce rendimientos bajos, lo que explica su precio elevado. El café estándar de Boquete (Typica, Caturra, Catuaí) es de muy buena calidad pero con un perfil más convencional. En los tours de café, pide específicamente la cata de Geisha: vale los $10–$15 extra del paquete premium.

- **$5** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Qué es el Mariposario Butterfly Haven y dónde está?**  
  Es un jardín cerrado de mariposas tropicales en vuelo libre, ubicado en la Avenida Principal de El Valle, a unos 400 metros del zoológico. Tiene entrada separada (alrededor de $5 para extranjeros) y abre de 8:00 am a 4:00 pm todos los días. Las mariposas están más activas entre las 9 am y las 2 pm.
- **$4.50  $2–3** `[schema]` — **FAQ — ¿Cómo llegar al zoológico desde Ciudad de Panamá?**  
  En bus desde la Terminal de Albrook (aproximadamente $4.50, 2 horas hasta El Valle) y luego taxi local por $2–3. En carro propio, toma la Interamericana y desvíate en Las Uvas hacia El Valle — son unos 28 km de carretera de montaña bien asfaltada. El viaje total desde la ciudad es de 2 horas.
